# Scenario catalog — mail-archive-tool

Per assurance-kit `process/scenario-catalog.md`. The tables here are the machine
contract the covers-map meta-test (`assureblock/covers_map_test.go`) parses — do
not restyle them. The tests encode the invariants; a change that cannot satisfy
an invariant is the thing that is wrong.

## 1. ID prefix

`MA-` (test-spec IDs). Invariants use the `R` series.

## 2. Invariants (what must always hold)

- **R1 — No silent loss.** Every message the reader yields is exported, and no
  attachment/inline image is silently dropped: content referenced but absent
  (not downloaded, unparseable, torn) is recorded durably in the manifest —
  classed *fillable* (an on-demand source may still deliver it) or *terminal*
  (a complete-at-fetch source never will) — and listed in the verification
  report regenerated from the manifest on every run, never hidden. Incremental
  runs revisit every fillable gap and fill it once the source holds the content.
- **R2 — Incremental idempotence.** An incremental re-run over an unchanged
  source exports zero new items; `full` re-exports all. The manifest is the sole
  dedup authority.
- **R3 — Stable identity; one copy per mailbox on the live path.** A message's
  dedup key is stable across runs (Internet Message-ID when present,
  deterministic content hash otherwise). A message captured from a LIVE source
  (Graph; IMAP later) is stored once per mailbox, keyed by identity: its folder
  over time is recorded (in the record's Folder/FirstFolder and the history log),
  and the current/served view groups it under its current — or a chosen date's —
  folder. A one-shot LOCAL import (PST/mbox/maildir) keeps the folder-scoped key,
  so the same mail filed in two folders exports to both, but never twice within
  one folder. Genuine simultaneous two-folder membership observed in one run is
  recorded for that date.
- **R4 — Containment.** Every path the exporter writes stays inside the output
  root. Store/folder/attachment names derived from untrusted mail cannot
  traverse out (`..`, absolute paths, path separators, reserved device names) or
  collide destructively.
- **R5 — Crash-safe state.** The manifest and every exported `.html`/`.zip` are
  written to a temp file and renamed into place (fsynced first); an interrupted
  or failed run never corrupts the manifest, never leaves a partial exported file
  under its final name, and never records a file that was not fully written;
  stale temps and orphan zips are swept; progress already made survives.
- **R6 — Faithful structure.** Output mirrors the source folder tree; each store
  gets its own top-level directory; a single mbox/maildir folder does not
  double-nest under its own name.
- **R7 — Self-contained, legible HTML.** Each exported `.html` renders the
  message body offline: inline `cid:` images are embedded as data URIs; header
  fields are HTML-escaped. The page stands on its own for a reader with no
  tool: it links back to its folder page and the archive root, lists its
  archived attachments with a link to the sibling zip, shows Message-ID,
  Sent/Received (with their original UTC offsets), Reply-To and Bcc, and links
  the preserved original `.eml` when one was kept. Folder pages paginate (never
  truncate) and label their dates UTC; the archive root carries a README.txt
  that explains the layout without the tool.
- **R8 — Search ↔ export parity.** The index holds exactly the exported
  messages; a full-text query returns matching items; free-text is matched as
  literal terms (no FTS operator injection or query crash).
- **R9 — Read-only source.** Reading a mail store never modifies the source
  mailbox. The only exceptions are opt-in local sync/write paths that never alter
  mailbox content: `-enable-offline` edits Thunderbird's `prefs.js` (backs up
  first, refuses while the app is running), and `-outlook`'s pre-copy
  Send/Receive lets Outlook refresh its own cache. Graph capture is GET-only.
- **R10 — Robust parsing.** Malformed/truncated input (bad mbox framing,
  non-MIME message, unparseable node) is skipped or fallback-parsed — never a
  fatal crash of the whole run. Damaged input (also a damaged folder table, a
  cyclic folder hierarchy, a special file) costs the smallest unit the tool can
  isolate: the message, else the folder; some faults cost a larger unit, and
  only that unit — a failed listing of a store's root costs that store, a failed
  enumeration of Graph folders that mailbox, an unreadable PST sub-folder table
  that sub-tree. A fault that surfaces as an error or a recoverable panic never
  hangs the run.
- **R11 — Correct date semantics.** `-since` parsing is total (relative +
  absolute + rejects garbage); the date filter excludes exactly the items
  outside the window.
- **R12 — Refusal legibility.** Invalid operator input is refused with a typed
  non-zero exit and a message naming the problem — never a panic or stack trace.
- **R13 — Reindex reconciles to disk.** `reindex` reconciles the archive to what
  is on disk: every indexed message whose exported file has been deleted, moved,
  or renamed is pruned from both the search index and the manifest; surviving
  files stay searchable; the browsable folder pages regenerate from the
  reconciled set; no exported message file is deleted — only stale
  `.mailarchive-*.tmp` temps and orphan `-attachments.zip` files with no sibling
  `.html` are swept (R5, MA-69).
- **R14 — Schedule is correct, opt-in, and reversible.** `schedule` generates a
  correct scheduler entry for the host OS (cron/launchd/Task Scheduler) carrying
  exactly the operator's backup job (an export, `graph` or `reindex` job),
  validated at schedule time through the job's own flag definitions: what
  cannot run unattended (interactive prep, a non-backup verb, `-outlook` off
  Windows, a Graph job without a secret file) is refused now. It is correct for
  any host path (Windows runs the job through a batch wrapper with every token
  quoted, so the 261-character run-string limit and cmd.exe metacharacters
  cannot bite); the job writes a size-capped log; one schedule per archive by
  default (the name derives from the archive path) and an archive-local
  descriptor names it. It prints the entry by default and only applies it
  under `--install`; `--install` is idempotent (re-running yields one entry) and
  `--remove` cleanly reverses it (entry, wrapper, descriptor), leaving
  unrelated entries untouched.
- **R15 — Evolution stores read faithfully.** An Evolution store is read without
  silent loss: the local Maildir++ store's dot-encoded, `_XX`-escaped folder
  hierarchy is decoded (every subfolder read, no traversal out of the root), and
  an IMAP disk cache's per-folder maildirs are all walked, whether nested
  directly or under a `subfolders` container. Each cached message is yielded once
  with its correct folder path.
- **R16 — Outlook COM export is opt-in and safe.** On Windows with *classic*
  Outlook, `-outlook` (and the GUI's Outlook-app option) drives Outlook to write
  a fresh, standard `.pst` per mail account, which then archives through the
  normal pipeline; every created PST file name stays inside the output root. On
  any platform without classic Outlook it refuses with a legible message naming
  the requirement — never a crash. (COM behaviour is lab-validated; go-pst
  cannot read every live `.ost`, so this is the reliable path for Exchange.)
- **R17 — Graph capture is complete, read-only, incremental.** The Microsoft
  Graph source — **app-only OR delegated per-user (`-auth app|device`)** —
  enumerates every folder (including nested) — excluding the
  Deleted Items and Junk Email subtrees by default, which `-include-deleted` /
  `-include-junk` restore (the exclusion is by resolved well-known-folder id, so
  it is locale-independent — T7, operator ruling) — and every message in the
  walked folders and archives each via its raw MIME through the shared parser; it issues
  only GET requests (**app-only holds the read-only `Mail.Read` application
  permission and addresses `/users/{upn}`; delegated holds delegated read-only
  `Mail.Read` for the signed-in user's OWN mailbox and addresses `/me`** — either
  way a mailbox is never modified); and an incremental re-run archives
  zero new items and re-downloads no already-archived message body, whichever mode
  captured it (the store key is seeded from the mailbox id, not the auth mode). A cross-folder
  Internet-Message-ID hit costs only a Message-ID membership check (no body
  fetched) and downloads only a Message-ID not yet archived anywhere in the
  mailbox; an already-archived message that merely moved between folders is
  recorded as a folder change with no download (a distinct message that REUSES an
  archived id is the bounded #8 residual on this floor build — skipped without
  capture, closed by the deferred Graph ImmutableId closure). A message a run
  could not archive is recorded as a capture gap and retried by every run whose
  scope covers it.
- **R18 — Every scheduled run is recorded and `status` reports it.** A run
  writes a `running` record the moment it holds the archive lock and finalizes
  it (ok / failed / cancelled, with counts and error) on the way out; `status`
  reports the archive's completeness, last run and schedule posture as
  GREEN/WARN/RED, failing closed on missing evidence (no record, an unreadable
  record, a run that never finished, a missing or moved scheduled program, a
  scheduler that cannot be queried), every WARN/RED naming its remedy; it exits
  0 whenever it reports. The GUI's job file round-trips the wizard's answers.
- **R19 — Offline-inert archive.** Every exported `.html` is safe to open from
  disk: its `<head>` begins with a Content-Security-Policy meta identical to the
  policy `serve` sends (no scripts, no remote loads, no `<base>`, no forms) and a
  no-referrer meta, placed before any mail-supplied markup; mail-supplied
  `refresh` and `content-security-policy` metas are neutralized. `serve` sends
  that policy for archived files, a `script-src 'self'` policy for its own UI,
  HTML-escapes search snippets, never follows a symlink out of the archive root,
  and warns when bound to a non-loopback address.
- **R20 — Extract is faithful-or-absent and contained.** `extract` migrates the
  archive out as standard interchange — mboxrd (one file per folder) or
  byte-exact `.eml` (one per message, mirroring the tree) — by copying only each
  record's PRESERVED original bytes (`<stem>.eml`, kept at capture with `-raw`);
  it never synthesizes or re-serializes a message, so a record with no preserved
  bytes (every PST/OST item; any archive captured without `-raw`) is counted,
  listed and skipped, never written. Each `.eml` is confirmed through the gate
  `verify` uses — a validated relative path resolved component-wise, no symlink
  followed at any level, regular-file-only, the read bounded to the recorded
  size — and, when a `Fixity.EML` is recorded, verified against it before
  emitting; a mismatch is skipped like an absence. Output is atomic and
  idempotent (a temp file per message/folder renamed on success, truncating a
  pre-existing file never appending, so a re-run does not double) and contained
  within `-dest`, which may not equal, sit inside, or contain `-out`
  (symlink-resolved). mbox is mboxrd (a reader that unquotes `>From ` recovers
  the exact bytes); `-format eml` is byte-exact. extract writes nothing into the
  archive, holds the archive's exclusive lock for its whole run, and is not a
  schedulable backup. Its exit is `0` (whole set emitted), `3` (partial — some
  records had no preserved bytes, including an archive from which nothing is
  extractable) or `1` (refusal/error). Capture and `verify` warn when a
  raw-capable source is archived WITHOUT `-raw`, so the dependency is visible
  before the source is deleted.
- **R21 — Point-in-time truth (go-back).** The served point-in-time view shows
  exactly the messages present on disk projected to the chosen date: `serve`
  renders the current mailbox (each message under its current folder, departed
  messages hidden) and any `?at=D` (the append-only history log folded to D, each
  message under its then-folder), each intersected with the files actually on
  disk — so a message removed from the archive (its files deleted, then
  `reindex`) never appears at ANY date, whether or not the log has yet been
  compacted. The history log is append-only and reindex-compactable: `reindex`
  drops the events of a removed message so redaction spans all dates, while a
  message merely departed from the live mailbox keeps its file, its manifest row
  and its timeline (R13). The go-back page is fully server-rendered and
  script-free (R19); a hostile folder name, subject or `?at` folded into it is
  inert, escaped text and can neither inject markup nor surface an off-disk
  message. When the log is missing or damaged, `serve` announces go-back
  unavailable/partial rather than silently showing current-only, and `status`
  reports the timeline's coverage and flags a torn tail (GREEN/WARN/RED with a
  remedy, X6).
- **R29 — Copy export is separate, contained, faithful-or-absent and
  self-verifying.** An Extraction-workspace export reads Microsoft 365
  (GET-only, the signer's own mailbox) or an archive, the configured one or
  another archive folder the user chooses (read-only, never locked, never
  written) [ruling W1]. It writes ONLY inside a newly created export directory
  under the user-chosen destination, plus its own 0600 run record in the local
  state directory (XC1). It never writes into an existing export, except to
  finish this app's own interrupted run when the root's identity matches its run
  record (XC2). It never writes into the archive. Every name derived from mail
  is sanitized, contained (`os.Root`) and case-insensitively distinct. Each
  message's original is copied byte-exact or recorded absent, never
  synthesized. Derived files are labelled derived. A manifest lists every
  selected message and file with its SHA-256, and `SHA256SUMS.txt` lets a
  recipient verify with stock tools. A preview that writes nothing precedes
  every run and binds it. A run that does not finish keeps its
  `_EXPORT-NOT-FINISHED.txt` marker. One message's failure never stops the run
  and is listed with its reason.
- **R23 — Local web surfaces admit only their launching account.** `serve`,
  `setup`, the desktop dashboard and reader validate `Host` on every request and
  admit only a browser holding the per-launch session obtained through a one-time
  link the process opened or printed; refused requests get a legible page with no
  archive data; a desktop launch reaches only its own account's verified
  instance. Not a defence against a process running as the same account.
- **R25 — Source-read containment.** Reading a local store (an mbox or Thunderbird
  store, a maildir, an Evolution store) never follows a directory link (a symlink,
  or on Windows a junction or mount point), never reads a special file (a pipe,
  socket or device), and never reads a file whose link target lies outside the
  store's resolved root, unless that input's directory-link opt-in is set; then its
  directory links are followed under a visited (dev, inode) set, and file links out
  of the store and special files are still refused. It covers every file a reader
  or discovery opens, so discovery cannot hang on a pipe before the lock is taken.
  Links are judged lexically, by `readlink`, before anything is resolved. Every
  refused entry that might hold mail is recorded in the capture-gap ledger (never
  only logged, never classed info); only a proven non-mail special file is info.
  Conditions: proven on Unix. On Windows it rests on the reparse-point classifier
  (an OS-independent test over synthetic file modes, plus an unprivileged junction
  case); a cloud placeholder is read, and that case is lab-pending (MA-503). It
  assumes a static hostile store that no other local user can write: a same-user
  process racing a link swap between the check and the open, and a co-writer who
  plants a hard link, are out of scope.
- **R26 — Parse fidelity.** Every MIME leaf part and every PST attachment the
  reader meets is either archived or recorded as a parse gap, and no part's failure
  ends the parse of another.
  - *Archived:* an attachment as its transfer-decoded bytes as the source store
    holds them, never charset-converted, under its own name (a truncated part
    under `<name>.partial`); a body text part decoded to valid UTF-8 for the page
    and the index, with every inline text part kept in document order.
  - *Recorded:* a parse gap naming the part, in the manifest, the report and on
    the page, with its bytes kept as received where they exist. The gap is
    terminal where re-reading cannot change the answer, and fillable on an
    on-demand source where a later complete copy can.
- **R27 — Frozen identity, guarded repair.** A raw-MIME message's dedup identity,
  fingerprint, content digest and file stem are computed from the frozen v1
  parser's view of it, and a PST item's from its v1 attachment names. No parser
  change therefore moves an archived message's key or fingerprint. A record
  written by an older or revoked parser scheme whose message now triggers a later
  scheme's fix is re-captured through R28's commit, rewriting it at most once per
  scheme and source content; its files are those that commit gives it (its stem,
  or that stem's `-vN` successor). That happens only when:
  - the archived files are present and as recorded;
  - it is provably the same message;
  - the re-capture is never to less (R28): the source still delivers every
    archived item, matched by content, and the new capture holds at least what v1
    does.

  Otherwise the record is left as archived, its timeline is still stamped, and the
  reason is shown.
- **R28 — Re-capture is monotone.** A re-capture of an already-archived message
  never makes an item the archive holds — the body, an attachment, an inline
  image, the preserved original — empty, unreadable or absent. It deletes an
  earlier file only after no persisted manifest record or index row references
  it. Folder pages are a regenerated view, not a durable reference: they follow
  the manifest's `PagesStale` flag. Exceptions, stated here rather than elsewhere:
  - *Body:* its kind and length are not ranked; only its presence is compared.
  - *Changed size:* same-name attachments whose non-empty size changed are not
    judged; the capture commits and counts them as `changed`.
  - *Legacy-scheme adopt (DEMOTE):* a regressed legacy-scheme adopt commits, and
    its earlier file is kept on disk and owned through the new record's
    `AlsoFiles`, rather than recorded as kept.
  - *HEAL:* a re-capture whose staged bytes equal the recorded fixity may rename
    them over a missing or damaged earlier unit in place.

  A capture that would degrade is not committed (KEEP: the earlier files stay and
  nothing is written), or is merged with the held items carried forward (MERGE).
  Either way it is recorded on the record as *kept* and shown by the run summary,
  the report, `status` and `verify`. The scope is a re-capture through the shared
  exporter commit — a full run, a fill retry or a legacy re-examination, on a
  local or a live source — and so also the MIME-fidelity repair (R27).
- **R33 — One program.** Every function the product offers is a verb of one
  executable (`mailarchive`), reachable from the command line, and every schedule
  and shortcut the product installs names that executable (on Windows, the
  GUI-subsystem build `mailarchivew.exe` when it is installed beside the program
  under that name, with the same owner and the same build (§4.6.0); otherwise the
  running file, which `status` names) (SC12). The program never starts a copy of
  itself to perform a function, and no surface exists only in a file that is not
  the program. `status` tells a former name of the program from a moved or
  missing one, naming the repair. Proven through MA-610..MA-616 and MA-628;
  "never starts a copy of itself" is proven by MA-612's walk of every
  process-start site and its recording fakes (SC52). Open condition (owner
  rulings rule 3): a program path passed through a variable into an opener's
  argument is not traced by the walk (§12) (SC52). If L-W2 shows that a wrapper
  does not wait for the GUI-subsystem child, pass its stderr or pass its exit
  code, the parenthesis gains "a wrapped schedule records the console build"
  (SC22): at SE0 if L-W2 has run by then; after SE0 that is a narrowing of landed
  text, which goes to the owner under rule 2.

- **R30 — Thunderbird pre-sync is opt-in, and inert when off.** The Thunderbird
  pre-sync (start Thunderbird out of sight, wait for its mail files to go quiet,
  ask it to quit, then archive) runs only when its per-schedule setting is on.
  With the setting absent or false no code of it runs: no process is listed, no
  profile is read, no file is written, the `app.Run` BeforeRead hook is nil, and
  every surface defaults OFF. Design `docs/design-thunderbird-presync.md` P1;
  proven through the pre-sync MA rows (MA-550.., MA-580).
- **R31 — Only what we started, and only by asking.** The tool asks Thunderbird
  to quit only for a process this session started and has identified (executable
  path, the `-profile` argument, and a start time at or after the launch),
  re-checked immediately before the request. It never signals, terminates or
  kills a process, never edits, deletes or moves any Thunderbird file, sends no
  keystrokes, and drives no window beyond the application's own quit request; a
  Thunderbird already running when the session began is neither started nor quit.
  A hard invariant: no OPEN owner question may be answered by weakening it.
  Design P2; proven through the pre-sync MA rows.
- **R32 — Pre-sync never gates the archive, and is always bounded.** No outcome
  of the pre-sync can stop or fail the archive run, which only reads: every
  failure ends in a recorded, named warning and the archive runs anyway (the one
  stop is cancellation), and every wait has a bound. A hard invariant: no OPEN
  owner question may be answered by weakening it. Design P3; proven through the
  pre-sync MA rows.
- **R37 — An encrypted archive's keys fail closed.** An encrypted archive's
  master key is stored only wrapped — under the password and under the recovery
  key, each through Argon2id at no less than the floor cost — in an authenticated
  vault (`.mailarchive-vault.json` + its `.bak` mirror), and, when the owner
  chooses "remember the key", as itself in the OS secret store under the
  archive's stable id. Every sealed object is XChaCha20-Poly1305 under a fresh
  random nonce, with associated data naming the format, the archive and the
  object's exact place. A wrong secret, an altered vault, a below-floor or
  above-ceiling cost, a superseded secret, or a remembered key that does not open
  the archive is refused with a typed, named error and writes nothing; a vault
  rewrite is atomic, durable, mirrored, generation-ordered and serialised by the
  archive-folder lock, and never rolls the vault back; a recovery key nobody has
  seen is never committed; and an archive this machine recorded as encrypted
  never reads as plaintext because its vault went missing. Design
  `docs/design-encrypted-archives.md` (Re-review amendments A1–A14, Phase-1
  adversarial pass amendments A2/A3/A7/A15; Phase 2a, A16–A20); proven through
  MA-703..MA-720 —
  for the pure core (Phase 1). The A15 half ("never reads as plaintext") is
  proven for the gate only until the Phase 2–4 write paths are wired; each of
  them owes an engine-level row that a recorded-encrypted archive with its
  vault deleted is refused with zero plaintext bytes written. Phase 2a's
  keyless paths carry it in MA-728 (MA-721..MA-733 cover Phase 2a).
- **R38 — An attended sync is only one the person started, at this computer.**
  Thunderbird is started or closed without the owner-activity checks only by a
  run the person started and is watching at this computer (Desktop Run now; the
  command line at a local foreground terminal without `-unattended`, not over
  SSH). The mode is never persisted; no scheduled run, remote session, job file,
  descriptor or archive file can turn it on. Every other R31 check holds in it,
  and every quit, in any mode, goes through the one pre-quit function. Design
  `docs/design-presync-attended.md` §2, §11 (accepted by the operator
  2026-10-30, OD-2; landed at slice AS0). Its MA rows land with the slices that
  add their tests (C-42): MA-895, MA-927 and MA-929 (slice AS1b); MA-898, its
  negative half — no scheduled run, job file, descriptor or command line is
  attended (slice AS3; the Run now halves land with AS4 and AS8).

## 3. Scenarios

| Scenario | Must hold | Recovery/response | Proven by |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 Untrusted mail names a file `../../x` or a folder `..` | R4 | name neutralized to a safe in-root segment | MA-01, MA-03, MA-29, MA-30 |
| S2 Run interrupted mid-export | R5, R2 | manifest intact; no partial html/zip visible; orphans swept; already-written items survive; resume skips them | MA-11, MA-22, MA-69, MA-94 |
| S3 Re-run over an unchanged source | R2 | zero new exports | MA-22 |
| S4 Same email filed in two folders (one-shot local import) | R3 | exported to both; never twice in one | MA-09, MA-13 |
| S5 Message with an inline `cid:` image | R7 | embedded as a data URI; renders offline | MA-19 |
| S6 Attachment/inline content not present locally | R1, R2 | skipped from the zip; recorded in the manifest (fillable or terminal) and the regenerated report; a fillable gap is re-examined by incremental runs and filled once the content is there; a legacy manifest migrates to "unknown" and is re-examined once | MA-20, MA-21, MA-66, MA-67, MA-68, MA-70, MA-71 |
| S7 Malformed or non-MIME message | R10, R1 | fallback-parsed or skipped; run continues | MA-13, MA-31 |
| S8 Search box contains FTS operators/quotes | R8 | treated as literal terms; no crash, no injection | MA-23, MA-32 |
| S9 Operator omits `-out`, gives a bad `-mode`, or serves a missing index | R12 | typed non-zero refusal naming the problem; no panic | MA-08, MA-33, MA-34 |
| S10 `-enable-offline` while the mail app is running | R9 | refused; prefs.js untouched (no backup written) | MA-27, MA-35 |
| S11 Reading a mail store | R9 | source bytes unchanged after a full read | MA-36 |
| S12 A store name/segment resolves to empty after sanitizing | R4, R6 | falls back to a stable placeholder, still in-root | MA-01 |
| S13 Operator deletes/renames exported files, then runs `reindex` | R13 | dangling index+manifest entries pruned; survivors searchable; pages regenerate | MA-40, MA-41, MA-42, MA-43, MA-44 |
| S14 Operator schedules a recurring backup, then re-runs / removes it | R14 | correct host-OS entry generated; install idempotent; remove reverses; unrelated entries kept | MA-45, MA-46, MA-47, MA-48, MA-49, MA-50 |
| S15 Evolution local Maildir++ store with nested, dot-encoded subfolders | R15, R1, R6 | every subfolder decoded and read; escaped names cannot traverse out | MA-52, MA-53, MA-55 |
| S16 Evolution IMAP disk cache: folders/<f>/{cur,new}, nested directly and via subfolders/ | R15, R1 | all folders walked; messages read as RFC822; single source | MA-54, MA-55 |
| S17 Auto-discovery hits an orphaned/corrupt Outlook `.ost` (removed account); or one locked by a running Outlook | R1 | unparseable stub excluded from the discovered set; a locked-but-valid file is kept | MA-56 |
| S18 A corrupt/truncated `.pst`/`.ost` (orphaned stub, bad OST variant) that panics go-pst | R10 | `Open` fails with a clean error; no panic escapes; the run continues and the no-console GUI never exits silently | MA-57 |
| S19 A live Exchange/IMAP `.ost` go-pst can't read; operator uses `-outlook` / the GUI Outlook-app option | R16 | on Windows, Outlook writes a clean `.pst` per account that archives normally; off Windows it refuses legibly | MA-58, MA-59, MA-60 |
| S20 Server-side archive of a (large) M365 mailbox via an app-only Graph grant, or a per-user delegated (device-code) grant against the operator's OWN mailbox | R17, R2, R9 | full first capture; incremental re-run adds nothing and re-downloads nothing; read-only; delegated mode signs in once by device code, addresses `/me`, caches a refresh token for later silent runs, archives only the signer's mailbox, and continues the SAME archive an app-only run started | MA-61, MA-62, MA-63, MA-64, MA-251, MA-252, MA-253, MA-254, MA-255, MA-256, MA-257, MA-258, MA-259, MA-260, MA-261 |
| S23 Two distinct messages share one Message-ID in one folder (a bug or a reused id) | R3, R1 | both exported and recorded (the second under a content-qualified key); an incremental re-run exports zero; names stay stable across full re-runs in any order | MA-86 |

| S33 The search index (search.db) is lost, corrupted, or left out of a copy, but the manifest and the archived `.html`/`.eml`/`.zip` files survive; the operator runs `reindex -rebuild` | R13, R8, R5, R4 | the index and folder pages are reconstructed from the archive alone (no source, no network): a record with a preserved `.eml` is parsed from those bytes, otherwise its fields are re-derived from the archived page (searchable text, not the wire bytes); the fresh index is built into a temp db and renamed over search.db only on success, carrying no orphaned FTS row; a record whose file is missing or fails the path gate is pruned-and-reported and never read (`..`/absolute/symlinked-component/oversized skipped); a per-record read/parse/zip error is isolated, never fatal; the summary reports the from-eml vs re-derived split and the unrecovered-field count; plain `reindex` on a lost index names `reindex -rebuild`, and rebuild with no manifest refuses naming `.mailarchive-manifest.json` | MA-171, MA-172, MA-173, MA-174, MA-175, MA-176 |

| S47 A local store has a hostile or odd layout: links out of the store, FIFOs, a cyclic hierarchy | R10, R25 | a cyclic or over-deep PST folder hierarchy is walked under a visited-NID set and a depth cap, so it neither overflows the stack nor loops: a folder whose id points back at an already-walked folder is a terminal `folder-cycle` gap, one nested past the cap a terminal `folder-too-deep` gap, and every other folder is archived once (the PST half, FI-S2a); a local store's directories are held to the same cap through every descent, so a chain of thousands of nested plain directories is cut at the cap with one terminal `folder-too-deep` gap on the folder where the walk stopped, and the walk stays quick (the local half, FI-S2b). A maildir, mbox or Evolution store is read under R25: a pipe, socket or device is never opened (an info `not-a-message-file` row, so a FIFO cannot hang a run or discovery); a link to a file outside the store is a terminal `symlink-outside-store` gap and its content is never read; a directory link is a terminal `symlinked-folder-skipped` gap whose remedy names the input's directory-link opt-in (`-follow-links`), so a link loop reads each message once, not many times; with the opt-in the input keeps its token and its directory links are followed under a visited (dev, inode) set (FI-S2b) | MA-317 (PST), MA-322 (links and special files), MA-503 (a cloud placeholder on Windows, pending) |

| S55 A pre-sync run was killed, could not close Thunderbird, or left a write-ahead record, and a later run reads that record | R31, R32 | a crash never leaves a hidden Thunderbird quit by a stale record and never costs the owner their own Thunderbird: a process matched exactly to the record is announced every run while it stays open and never quit in v1 (Q4); one that cannot be matched is left alone; a reboot clears the record; the state record is atomic, bounded, tolerant of unknown fields, and holds no hostname or wall-clock deadline | MA-554, MA-558, MA-565 — landing with slices TP1/TP2 |

| S58 An older binary meets a schedule, job file or last-run record carrying the new pre-sync fields, or a newer binary meets an old one | R32 | the descriptor, job-file and last-run fields are additive and ignored by older readers (the run simply has no sync); an older CLI handed the new flags refuses with "flag provided but not defined" and records the run failed (no silent skip); `status` flags a configured schedule whose last run has no pre-sync record (`presync_not_recorded`); no version bump forces an old GUI to refuse the whole backup | MA-551 — landing with slice TP2; MA-916 (the attended sync's records, both ways, from golden v1.5.1 bytes — design-presync-attended slice AS7) |
| S60 An owner encrypts an archive, later changes the password or forgets it and uses the recovery key, while a crash, a tampered copy, an older MailArchive or an unattended backup without a usable key meets the archive | R37 | the password and the recovery key each open it and nothing else does; a password change or new recovery key re-wraps only the vault (no archive byte re-encrypted, the remembered key untouched); a crash mid-rewrite leaves a copy the newest authentic generation is chosen from, and the superseded password is refused rather than used to roll back; an altered vault is refused; an older MailArchive meets a cleartext manifest envelope and asks to be upgraded instead of suggesting deletion; an unattended run whose key cannot be retrieved refuses before writing; a deleted or swapped vault under an archive this machine recorded as encrypted is a refusal, never a plaintext export; a recovery key is committed only after it was shown | MA-703..MA-718 — the pure core (Phase 1); engine, UX and unattended wiring land in later phases |
| S64 The owner clicks Run now with Thunderbird sync on (plaintext or encrypted), at the computer, or cancels, or locks | R30, R31, R32, R38, R37 | Thunderbird starts out of sight unless already open (on Windows: minimized to the taskbar, its window possibly shown for a moment [OQ-2]), the note shows each phase and the outcome, the person can keep Thunderbird open, or stop the wait and back up what has arrived [OQ-6], Cancel asks the Thunderbird it started to close (where the platform's attended close is allowed; otherwise it is left open and the person told [OQ-1]), Lock on an encrypted archive cancels it at any point before mail reading starts and clears the key [OQ-3] (as it does for every encrypted Run now, sync or not [OQ-8]) | MA-928 (its slice-AS1a part: the Windows launch's working folder); MA-895, MA-896, MA-897, MA-922 (the session), MA-925 (the session and its line), MA-929 (the session) — slice AS1b; MA-902 (a cancel before mail reading starts stops the run before it reads) — slice AS2; MA-898 (the plaintext Run now attended), MA-906, MA-914, MA-915 (the AS4 texts), MA-922 (the Desktop), MA-928 (the Windows start details), MA-929 (the Desktop button and route) — slice AS4; MA-898 (the encrypted Run now attended), MA-911 (the encrypted sync-only box), MA-912 (`api/runnow-sync`), MA-913 (Lock before mail reading, every encrypted Run now) — slice AS8; the rest land with the slice that adds each test — design-presync-attended §11, §12, C-42; landed at slice AS0 (accepted by the operator 2026-10-30, OD-2) |
| S65 Scheduled syncs keep being skipped, and the owner must hear of it | R32, X6, X10 | the outcome is recorded per run (plaintext), the Recent backups card tags it, Status warns after 3 days of scheduled runs with no sync with a remedy for the reason, one email per streak; encrypted archives record nothing beside the archive | MA-925 (its slice-AS1b part: the no-change outcomes and their line); MA-907 (its slice-AS3 part: every line a scheduled run writes to `mailarchive.log` is dated); MA-903 (the outcome on the plaintext record and history), MA-904 (its record parts: nothing beside an encrypted archive), MA-905 (the Recent backups tag), MA-916 (records across versions), MA-925 (the no-change tag) — slice AS7; MA-904 (its sync-streak part: plaintext only), MA-908 (the sync streak), MA-909 (the Status line, the 3-day WARN and its remedies, `status -json`), MA-920 (the streak's migration), MA-926 (the no-change count and its softer warning) — slice AS5; MA-910 (the email once per streak, the checkbox, `ClearStreak` and uninstall) — slice AS6; the rest — design-presync-attended §11, §12: each MA row lands with the slice that adds its test (C-42), from AS3; landed at slice AS0 (accepted by the operator 2026-10-30, OD-2) |

Acknowledged limits (not defects): two messages that reuse one Message-ID with
an identical envelope (subject, sender, recipients, date, attachment names) and
differ only in body are treated as one message (a resend); very large attachments are buffered whole in
memory (bounded by the largest single attachment, not the mailbox); the Graph
incremental fast-path recognises an already-archived message by Message-ID
MEMBERSHIP — no envelope signature — so an archived id is skipped without download
whatever folder it is now in (R17), and a distinct message reusing that id is the
bounded #8 residual on this floor build (skipped without capture, closed by the
deferred Graph ImmutableId closure; a DOWNLOAD path — local import or full run —
splits it by content fingerprint, R1), but a message with NO
Internet-Message-ID is deduped mailbox-wide on its post-download content hash and
so is re-downloaded on every run — its identity is only knowable once the body
arrives (the acknowledged no-mid price); every manifest record
carries a 16-hex content fingerprint (~16% growth) so that Message-ID reuse is
detected for local sources; PST message categories are read from go-pst's flat
`StringToID["Keywords"]` map, so a store that also defined an unrelated named
property called `Keywords` in another property set could shadow the categories
one (an unlikely, acknowledged limit), the multi-value parse is bounds-checked
and best-effort, and real categorized-PST validation is lab-pending (MA-197).
Recorded here so a finding against them is a design conversation, not a silent
gap.

## 4. Test specs

Tiers: **U** unit property (every commit) · **S** structural whole-tree walk
(every commit) · **A** fail-closed, infra-free · **L** lab (real infra).

| MA-450 | U | the Windows wrapper's run line, put through a cmd.exe model (%% expansion, unquoted `2>>` redirect stripped, no other unquoted metacharacter) and then Go's Windows os.Args parser, yields exactly the job's argv and the .stderr.log target for every host path — a drive root (`E:\`), a share root (`\\nas\backup\`), a trailing separator, doubled separators — never an escaped closing quote that merges later arguments | R14, S28 |
| MA-451 | U | a wrapper carrying non-ASCII paths (Jörg, Eingänge, Анна, 田中) — in every token, only in the -out/-log/`2>>` target beside an ASCII exe and -config (C:\Program Files), only in -input, or only in the exe (a per-user install under C:\Users\Jörg, with -out, -input and -log ASCII) — decoded the way cmd.exe reads a batch file (line by line in the active code page, starting from OEM CP437/850/866/932/936, UTF-8 after a `chcp 65001` line), still yields exactly the job's argv and .stderr.log target; no BOM, every byte read before the switch is ASCII, a non-ASCII byte anywhere on the run line switches, and an all-ASCII wrapper never switches the console code page | R14, S28 |
| MA-452 | U | a cron line whose exe/-out/-input/-log carries `%` (plain, URL-encoded, with a space, a quote, a backslash before the `%`, a doubled `%%`), put through cron's command-field rule (the first unescaped `%` ends the command) and a real sh word expansion, yields exactly the job's argv with nothing left over as stdin; the managed block's -out reads back as the archive path (a block written before `%` was escaped included) so the collision guard still recognises the archive's own block (Unix hosts only: cron never runs on Windows, and a Windows sh.exe re-splits the line under its own rules) | R14, S28 |
| MA-453 | U | `schedule`'s device-job sign-in check reads the same backend the unattended run loads from: with a token store (the Windows vault, where the CLI saves the sign-in and never writes the -token-cache file) the store decides, otherwise the file; CheckSignIn accepts exactly when an unattended NewDelegated with the same config loads a sign-in, so a vault-only sign-in is schedulable and an empty vault is refused naming the sign-in remedy even beside an old signed-in file, writing nothing; any other failure to read that backend (a vault this session cannot open — Credential Manager's ERROR_NO_SUCH_LOGON_SESSION outside an interactive logon, injected — a file readable by other users, a sign-in that is not valid JSON) is refused naming where the check looked (the credential store with the tenant and client id, or the file path), the cause with its own remedy, and the sign-in remedy, never a bare store error; on Windows the install-time checks run against a stand-in vault in deviceTokenStore's place, never the runner's real Credential Manager; the unattended device run resolves its sign-in through that same seam, for the job's tenant and client id: with the stand-in holding a sign-in that does not parse and no -token-cache file, runGraph fails on that vault entry exactly as the check does, never falling back to the file, writing nothing and reaching no network | R14, R12, S28, S20 |
| MA-454 | U | `schedule -remove -- verify -out X` (the verify install command with -install swapped for -remove) removes the archive's VERIFY entry, keeping its backup entry and the backup's descriptor; the plain `-out X -remove` still removes the backup with its descriptor; a verify removal that finds no entry under the archive's derived `-verify` name (the verify was installed under its own -name, as the old README's `-name mailarchive-verify`, or an older release's descriptor records the verify) exits 0, removes nothing (the backup, both descriptors byte-identical and the custom-named verify all stay), says "nothing to remove" naming the absent entry — never "no schedule was installed for X" — and hints to run it again with the -name the verify was installed with: the name the verify's own descriptor records (MA-457), else the verify an older release's backup descriptor records, else the generic `-name NAME` (a verify an earlier release installed is recorded nowhere); that hint followed removes just the verify (fake launchctl/crontab on PATH, sandboxed HOME) | R14, R18, S14, S28 |
| MA-455 | U | -remove deletes the archive's descriptor only with the schedule it belongs to: `-name <other entry> -out X -remove` keeps it; a name that is neither recorded nor installed removes nothing, says "nothing to remove" naming it, never touches the scheduler, and leaves the descriptor byte-identical; the descriptor's own name is removed with it; a descriptor that exists but cannot be read (bad JSON) is never deleted by any entry's removal (owner ruling, rulings §13.6, ESC-10, which narrows this row's earlier guarantee that it went with the archive's default-named schedule): it stays byte-identical when the archive's default-named backup (`-out X -remove`, the entry it falls back to), its verify, or a backup installed under a custom -name (`-name NAME -out X -remove`, since a backup under any -name writes it) is removed, whether through `schedule -remove` or the dashboard's Remove; each such backup removal (the file cut short, or naming a schedule outside the -name grammar) prints the file's path and the remedy (delete it if no backup schedule for this archive is installed, or re-run that schedule's -install), and `status` keeps reporting the file with the reader's cause ("is unreadable", "names an invalid schedule") and that remedy as a WARN, also in a folder with no manifest or run record yet, never "no archive" or "Schedule: none recorded"; the entry name a descriptor records is untrusted (a folder handed over can name any entry) and acts only when it binds to this archive: `-out X -remove`, the GUI's Remove and its Repair take the recorded name only if it is this archive's derived name, or a custom name whose job backs up this archive (or the folder it was moved from, which is gone), whose cron block's -out, or whose Windows task this tool installed (proven by the XML definition written for every install, or the wrapper — for an over-long command only — whose -out is then also matched; MA-650), is this archive; a name that is another archive's, a descriptor copied from an archive that still exists, a root-folder task of another program and a record from another host are each refused naming the descriptor, the name and the `-name` remedy, with the entry untouched; `status` prints no `-name N -remove` from a record that does not bind, and a custom name on launchd, and an entry of an archive that is only absent (an unplugged drive reads as moved), are open escalations (UJ-6, K1) | R14, R18, S14 |
| MA-456 | U | on cron hosts only (Linux/BSD), the one-archive-per-name guard covers a scheduled verify too: a verify for archive B under a name whose managed block already verifies archive A (the old README's fixed `-name mailarchive-verify`) is refused naming the name, A and the -name remedy, with the crontab not rewritten; A's own verify re-installs under that name, also when A's descriptor records its backup under another name (that guard stays backup-only). Not covered (an open residual, not accepted; encoded by a skipped test): launchd and Task Scheduler entries are not read back for any job (UJ-6, upheld by owner ruling FB-4), so on macOS and Windows a second archive's verify under a shared -name still replaces the first's at install; the archive that lost its verify shows `verify_stale` (MA-466) | R14, S28 |
| MA-457 | U | a scheduled verify records its own descriptor (owner ruling FB-4): installing a verify writes `.mailarchive-verify-schedule.json` beside `.mailarchive-schedule.json` with the same discipline (version 1; the verify's name, cadence, time, program and job; a 0600 owner-only file renamed into place, no temp left behind), names it on the install's Log line, and leaves the backup's descriptor byte-identical; a descriptor is written only after the host scheduler has the entry, so an install whose scheduler step fails (crontab refusing the new table, Task Scheduler refusing /Create, launchctl failing `load`, each through a stand-in) returns that error naming the step and writes neither descriptor, for a verify or a backup alike; each descriptor goes only with its own entry — removing the backup (`-out X -remove`) deletes the backup's descriptor and keeps the verify's entry and descriptor byte-identical, removing the verify (`-remove -- verify -out X`, or `-name NAME -out X -remove` for the name the verify descriptor records, reported as a verify) deletes the verify's and keeps the backup's; a verify the descriptor records but the scheduler no longer has (taken out by hand) still counts as present, so its removal clears the stale record — on Task Scheduler too, where a /Delete of a task already gone is no error once /Query no longer finds it, while a task it still has but cannot delete stays an error naming schtasks /Delete, with the record kept (the Windows removal driven on every OS against a fake Task Scheduler); a verify descriptor that cannot be read is never deleted, with either entry, and the verify's removal prints its path and remedy (delete it if no verify schedule for this archive is installed, or re-run that verify's -install); a verify installed under a -name other than the one the archive's records give its verify (the verify descriptor, else an older release's backup descriptor recording the verify) never removes that earlier entry, and while the host scheduler still has it the install names it with `mailarchive schedule -name OLD -remove -- verify -out X`, which, followed, removes only that entry and keeps the new verify's entry and descriptor; a first install, a re-install under the same name, or an earlier entry already gone from the scheduler names nothing; a verify descriptor is trusted only as a verify of this archive under a name of its own: one whose job is not a verify (a backup's descriptor copied over it), one recorded on this host for another folder (an archive copied with its records), or one recording the name of the archive's backup schedule (which the install refuses) reads as unreadable, naming the file, the cause and its remedy — status reports it so, the verify's removal never deletes it, and beside it `-out X -remove` reports and removes the backup only, its descriptor with it, leaving the planted file byte-identical — while a verify recorded on another host may name the folder as that host mounts it; on every OS the name guard reads the archive's own two descriptors, so a verify under the name the backup's descriptor records and a backup under the name the verify's records are each refused naming the name, the archive and the -name remedy, with the entries and descriptors unchanged (a descriptor an older release wrote for the verify itself is that verify's own record), and no launchd or Task Scheduler entry is read back (UJ-6); a verify recorded for a folder that no longer exists is the moved archive's own and stays readable (a folder that still exists is a copy, and reads as unreadable as above), and a recorded name that does not bind to this archive is never offered as the earlier verify, the removal hint or `status`'s clear command | R14, R18, S14, S28 |
| MA-458 | U | `verify_stale` is judged against the scheduled verify's own cadence, read from its own descriptor (owner ruling FB-4), never the backup's: with a daily backup and a weekly verify, a verify that finished 10 days ago is not stale and one that finished 15 days ago is, the WARN naming the weekly period, the verify schedule and a pasteable re-install of this archive's verify (its cadence, time and verify flags, no -name; an unparseable time left out), with a shared -name named as a cause only off cron hosts; with a weekly backup and a daily verify, 3 days is stale; a verify with no run recorded is judged from the descriptor's install time (3 days after a weekly install not stale, 20 days after stale; no install time, not judged); a last verify still recorded as running is judged from its start: one running for an hour with the archive lock held is not flagged, one started more than twice the period ago is stale ("started … ago", "has not finished") whether the lock is held or free, and one whose lock is free never finished — a WARN at once (`verify_never_finished`, a day after its start too) naming its start, its log and `verify -out`, so a verify that dies on every run is never GREEN; a verify schedule installed (or re-installed) after the last verify ran keeps `verify_stale` while two of its periods have not passed since the install (as MA-466 pins), but the WARN says the schedule was installed after the last verify and has not run since, naming its next run and `verify -out` now — never "stopped running" or a re-install, which would change nothing — and one installed longer ago with no verify since says no verify has run since the install, with the re-install remedy; a last-verify record that cannot be read is a WARN (`lastverify_unreadable`) naming the file and `verify -out`, never GREEN; older installs raise no false WARN — a verify an earlier release installed records no descriptor, so its staleness is not judged, while a verify an even older release recorded in the backup's descriptor is still judged against that record (MA-155), and the verify's own descriptor wins when both exist; a verify descriptor that cannot be read is a WARN (`verify_descriptor_unreadable`) naming the file and its remedy and gives no cadence; `status` prints a `Verify schedule:` line (name, state, cadence) and `status -json` carries `verify_schedule`, neither when no verify is recorded, and a folder whose only record is the verify's descriptor is reported, never "no archive" (real binary, sandboxed scheduler) | R18, R14, S29 |
| MA-466 | U | `status` watches a verify that `schedule` installed as it watches the backup (owner ruling FB-4; formerly the open R18/X6 gap N11): with a backup and a weekly verify installed through the real binary (sandboxed scheduler) and the last verify finished 30 days ago, `status -json` carries `verify_stale`; a verify that ran a day ago does not. The verify's cadence comes from its own descriptor (MA-457, MA-458), so the archive whose verify another archive's took over under a shared -name on launchd or Task Scheduler (FB-4's open residual, not detected at install) shows this WARN once its own verify stops running. As for the backup, the verify's entry itself is watched: on this host a verify recorded but absent from the scheduler is a WARN at once (`verify_not_installed`, naming the re-install and the `-name NAME -remove -- verify -out X` that clears the record; through the real binary, its entry taken out of the sandboxed scheduler by hand, a day after its last run), a scheduler that cannot be queried is `scheduler_unavailable` (once, beside the backup's), and a recorded program that is gone or is not this binary is `verify_exe_missing` or `verify_exe_moved`, naming the re-install — WARNs, never RED; a verify recorded on another host is `verify_other_host`, its state and program not judged from here; a healthy recorded verify raises none of these | R18, R14, S29 |

| MA-187 | U | mail may not wear the tool's private namespace: neutralize strips class="mailarchive-*" and data-mailarchive-* from every mail-supplied element so a hostile HTML body cannot inject a header marker, and reindex -rebuild's from-HTML reader anchors to the first <div class="mailarchive-header"> that is a direct child of <body> (never a <head> <template> or a body-nested div), so a crafted message cannot forge or erase its own indexed sender/subject/date; on a page v0.1.1–v0.2.3 wrote (no stripping; a full-document mail kept as the page with the header injected after its first `<body ...>` match) the header is anchored by those bytes (the first match, then exactly the old header) and trusted only where it is live directly in <body>, so a forged header div in the mail's <head>, which the parser moves into <body> ahead of the real one, is ignored and the record rebuilds with its real subject, sender and date (`from:ceo` finds nothing; a benign old full document is the twin) — end to end through Rebuild on on-disk-format fixtures. Nor can a current mail erase them by hiding its own page's header: the exporter writes no page whose header div and body div are not live directly in `<body>` in both scripting readings (MA-474, MA-476), so the reviewer's nine (adv_plaintext_head_{esc,lnk,ref}_{a,d} and adv_scriptesc_head_{esc,lnk,ref}_a, whose in-place noscript rewrite left the head `<noscript>` unclosed — unrecovered-fields=18 on the 372-vector corpus), a head `<script>` left in an escape state, and head `<noscript>`s holding a `<plaintext>`, a `<frameset>` or an element left open each keep the header and body live in both readings and rebuild with their own subject, sender and date (unrecovered 0), with the same hits live and rebuilt. OPEN GAP in the "cannot erase" half, NOT accepted (pending an operator decision): where a page carries the old header's bytes after its first `<body` match but they are not live in <body> — an old mail whose first `<body` sat in its <title>, or a CURRENT page whose head <style>, <noscript> or comment carries them — bytes cannot tell which header the renderer wrote (on an old full-document page every other byte is the mail's), so the reader reads neither: subject, From and date are counted unrecovered (erased, never forged) while the record keeps its body words, shallow and 509 deep (MA-482); the tests pin "never forged, counted honestly, body kept", not the erasure, and a SKIPPED test (TestRebuildCurrentPageRecoversRealHeaderDespiteMimicInHead) encodes the "cannot erase" invariant the ruling must restore for a new page — it fails if un-skipped today, and its Skip is removed when an out-of-band writer-version signal lands. SECOND OPEN GAP, NOT accepted (round 3, R3-AF2, pre-existing, display-level): the rebuild reverses the header's `Name <email>` by cutting at the LAST ` <`, which is not invertible when the address itself holds ` <` (net/mail decodes a quoted local part: `From: Mallory <"x <ceo"@bank.example>`), so the rebuilt sender is not the live one — a mail chooses its rebuilt sender_email, and with no display name the live address becomes the rebuilt name; fixing it changes the page's sender format (an owner decision), and a SKIPPED test (TestRebuildRecoversSenderWithAnEmbeddedAngleBracket) encodes it and fails if un-skipped today | R19, R8, R7, S33 |

| MA-314 | U | MIME and listing bounds (scaled: idle 200 ms, cap 5 s, size cap 1 MiB). A server trickling 1 byte/50 ms for 1.5 s (beyond a 1 s wall clock) completes. A server stalling >200 ms mid-body → `fetch-timeout` gap, and the walk ctx is still live (the next item is fetched). A server that keeps streaming past the **overall cap** → `fetch-timeout` [FC32]. A body over the size cap → `fetch-too-large`, terminal, nothing written [FC21]. **[RR-6 RR6-C3]** A body exactly at the scaled cap completes; one byte more is refused with nothing returned; a response whose `Content-Length` exceeds the cap is refused before its body is read. A repeating `nextLink` ends the folder as `folder-list-failed`. A repeating `nextLink` in **Folders** → the mailbox fails, the walk terminates. A `nextLink` to a foreign host → refused, no bearer token sent [FC21]. **[RR-6 RR6-C2]** These primitive cases pass the scaled overall cap explicitly; capture's own overall cap until S3 is `Config.MIMETimeout`, which MA-98 asserts unchanged. The shipped-path cases are MA-540 and the folder-hierarchy cases MA-541 [RR-6]. **Slices [ruling J4]:** the row lands with SG (the trickle, stall, overall-cap and size-cap primitive cases, the size-cap boundary and `Content-Length` cases [RR-6 RR6-C3], and the repeat stop in `Folders`); its `fetch-timeout`, `fetch-too-large` and `folder-list-failed` gap cases land with S3, and its capture foreign-host case with SGc; until then each is a skip encoder citing MA-314. **[RR-6 RR6-C3; re-verification]** While capture passes no size cap (owner ruling RR6-OR1's safe interim), the `fetch-too-large` gap case stays a skip encoder citing MA-314 | R10, R17, S46 (from S1a) |

| MA-663 | U | `archindex.Forget` removes exactly the named entry and no other (forgetting a non-member changes nothing), and the index is bounded: writing or reading more than `MaxEntries` is refused naming the limit (fail-closed, nothing written), while exactly `MaxEntries` round-trips (SD-8, S1-C5) | design §4, SD-8 |

The fix reads the inputs defensively (node-check + the real GUI confirm it); this row pins the DOM shape — a local archive's card renders Remove but not `schedInterval`/`schedTime`/`schedInstall` | design §4.6.3 |

| MA-693 | U | the headless status surfaces source-loss (archive-manager Slice 3c, S3-C6): when the archive-local source-loss streak has crossed the surface threshold, `health.Assess` adds a WARN naming the since-date and the remedy ("this archive's source has not been reachable since DATE — reconnect the source …"), so a CLI/headless operator reading `status` sees it, not only the Desktop banner. No streak, or one below the threshold, adds no such WARN. Proven: a source-lost archive reads WARN with the since-line; a below-threshold one does not. *Amended 2026-10-28 (design-desktop-settings-card §3.7, slice A4, R3C-9 accepted by the owner): the surface threshold is the unreachable runs spanning 3 days (at least two runs, never measured to now), not 3 runs; nothing weakened* | design §5.7 (S3-C6), design-desktop-settings-card §3.7 |

| MA-695 | U | Shut down from the page (archive-manager, tester request): the Desktop's `/api/shutdown` (loopback+CSRF) cancels the process lifecycle, which serveInstance watches (it now selects on `life.Context().Done()`, a cancellable child of the root context) to stop the dashboard and reader servers and release the account lock — so the next launch starts cleanly with no port conflict and nothing to find and kill by hand. `Lifecycle.Stop()` is the in-process twin of the signal/Ctrl-C path: it drains the running export within DrainBudget first, so a stop never half-writes or touches an archive (R36). | MA-696 | U | Add another archive from the page (archive-manager, tester request): the "Your archives" card gains an "Add another archive" button whose `/api/add-archive` (loopback+CSRF) runs the wizard's local add flow IN PROCESS — `wizard.AddArchive`, the same native-dialog flow the double-click wizard uses, factored out of `wizard()` as `localArchiveFlow` (no first-step surface, no scheduled-archive health check; desktop imports wizard, so it is never a child — MA-612). The new archive is folded into the known-archives index via the monotone `archindex.Reconcile` so it LISTS, but is never made current (an additional archive, not a switch); a cancel adds nothing; a second concurrent add is refused (the dialogs are process-global); it never touches an existing archive (R36). Proven (through the addArchiveFn seam): a created archive is registered and not switched to; a cancel registers nothing; a flow error is reported; a busy add is refused; registerArchive is monotone | design §13 (UX-1) |
| MA-697 | U | Built-in email alerts engine (archive-manager / St Augustine's request): `internal/alert` sends a backup-result email directly over SMTP in pure Go (net/smtp + crypto/tls) — NO outside program, so MA-612's closed OS-tool set is untouched; MailArchive composes and transmits the mail itself. Provider presets for Office 365 (smtp.office365.com:587 STARTTLS) and Gmail (smtp.gmail.com:587 STARTTLS) plus a standard-SMTP option (host/port/security: STARTTLS, implicit TLS on :465, or none). The SMTP password is NOT stored by the engine — the caller reads it from the OS secret store and passes it to Send, so it never reaches the settings file or a log. Alerts fire per the event gate (enabled + on failure / success / auto-pause). Message headers are CRLF-stripped so a crafted archive path or error cannot inject headers. | MA-700 | U | SMTP send is time-bounded (email-alerts, adversarial-pass finding): net/smtp and tls.Dial impose no timeout, so an unbounded send would let a stalling or malicious mail server hang the scheduled backup (fireAlert runs synchronously after the backup is on disk) or the alert-test handler goroutine forever. `smtpTransport` now dials with `net.Dialer{Timeout}` / `net.DialTimeout` and sets a connection deadline (`smtpSendTimeout`) over the whole SMTP conversation — the 220 greeting and every reply — in both implicit-TLS and STARTTLS paths; the timeouts are vars a test shrinks. two findings of one rank are each named with their own remedy (FID-06); a refused load opens no message and says so, its counts the keyless check's files, its remedy its own outcome's — a restore only for a damaged record (VERD-02, OUT-3); both copies of one part of the record gone is red (OUT-2); a chunk pass that ended early attests nothing (CRIT-04); a lost stretch of the go-back timeline or a search index behind its records, which no chunk shows, is amber (VERD-05); a message too large is counted and told truly (MailArchive's own limit), and one this computer could not read (access, a drive error) is amber, never a red that would stand (VERD-04, CRIT-05); a file both checks name is listed once, by the key's finding with its part (FID-04); a check with the key that finds only amber removes an earlier red notice (REC-03); status's tier-2 red and the attention file name `verify -full` (REC-05, CRIT-02); the standing line is worded by what the keyless check can see (REC-04); the stopped and never-finished notes and the CLI's stop line name `-full` for a check with the key (KEY-03, REC-07); `-full` refuses a busy archive before any key is asked, key flags on an archive that is not encrypted say so, and the help is true (FID-07, FID-08). On a read-only medium it runs without the archive lock, judging the record's pointers before and after, and a result it cannot save is told, the exit unchanged. `-full` on an archive that is not encrypted, `-full` with `-unattended`, a key flag without `-full` and a scheduled `verify -full` are each refused by name | design-encrypted-archives-phase3 §8, R37 |
| MA-798 | S | encrypted archives Phase 3, P3-2a, the adversarial pass's fixes (review-encrypted-archives-phase3-p32a-adversarial): `Reader.Close` — what serve and search call as they end — removes a scratch index's plaintext file before it returns (LEAK-01); locking the open a reader reads through drops its view, pages and scratch file as its own lock does (LEAK-02); Lock never waits on a load, and a load finishing after a lock is closed, never served (KEY-03); a folder link asked for after a lock gets the locked page (KEY-04); a file response's write deadline renews with each write, so a client that stops reading gives back its memory budget within it — an unlock's derivation and Lock are not held behind a stalled download (KEY-05); with no usable index the folder pages and search say why and name `reindex -rebuild`, never "could not be read" (FID-02); a history chunk gone or damaged is a partial go-back timeline, never an error, and go-back reads the sealed history and a past date (OUT-B, FID-06); a unit gone is loaded for again only when the record moved, and a unit whose bytes are not its record's never (OUT-D); a load that fails as the record changes is loaded again (FID-03); a lock between a unit's read and its decryption, or during a load, is the locked page (FID-04); an index behind is told on the search page (FID-05); the attachment zip is served from its own unit (FID-07); a message too large is named by its own size (FID-12); the API's answers are written after the index is released (CRIT-03); `search -paths` and a non-loopback `serve` are refused before the want of a key, and serve without one names the secret files (FID-08); a read-only load keeps the file-list rules, and the reader reads a folder it cannot write (FID-11); keyed `reindex -rebuild` stops between messages at Ctrl-C or SIGTERM, saving nothing, exit 130/143, a second signal at once (CRIT-01), and without a terminal says it needs one with the password, naming no remedy that does not exist (REG-02) | design-encrypted-archives-phase3 §3.2, §7.3, §7.4, §7.5, R37 |

| MA-793 | S | encrypted archives Phase 3, P3-1b, the adversarial pass's fixes (review-encrypted-archives-phase3-p31b-adversarial): `OpenWithin` judges every copy's cost before any derivation — the re-derivation on a newer copy too — and names the most each setting asks, so a wrong password beside a costlier copy derives nothing (CRY-01, REG-02, REG-03); the cost question never lets a raw `vault.CostError` out and a yes never lowers a setting (REG-02); at a recorded folder with no sealed record a restore is refused when a key file naming the archive is not the source's own — a forged recovery file's other master key — while one merely damaged, its recovery entry the file's own, is put back (CRY-02, CRIT-03); a copy claiming a generation within 2^32 of the cap is refused by its name, never wrapped round or used up silently, while a claim far ahead of the clock but not near the cap is outranked (CRY-03, OUT-03, FID-04, CRIT-02); once the primary is written a restore says it is in effect whatever follows — the read-back reads only the primary (a mirror planted after cannot undo it), a primary that does not read back is `vault.ErrUnconfirmed`, on the mirror-failed path too, and a record update that fails is `keyring.ErrRestoredNotRecorded` — and the command line then keeps the saved copies of the recovery key in force and says the key file is back (OUT-01, OUT-04, CON-02, CON-03, FID-02); a folder renamed away and replaced, by another folder or a link, between prepare and Commit gets nothing written (OUT-02); Commit re-reads the record — asking again about an archive forgotten meanwhile (CON-01) — and judges the folder's case again (CRIT-05); each refused state appearing after prepare makes Commit refuse, the clock check has its positive twin at this build's date, and a record pointer from a newer MailArchive or one that is not a pointer is refused (FID-03); at a folder this computer has not recorded a recovery file above MailArchive's own cost is asked about before it is derived (K14, CRY-05); a remembered-key restore does not hold other opens behind the secret store (CON-04); damaged key files at a recorded folder are offered the restore (FID-05); `vault.RecoveryEntrySource` and pending restores print redacted (CRIT-04); without `-recovery-file-out`, `new-recovery-key` and `restore-key-file` offer to save the new recovery file at the terminal, and without one the person is told to keep the earlier pair rather than destroy it (CRIT-01); `restore-key-file` checks the recovery file and the recovery key's typing before the new password, and asks again for a key that does not open the file, keeping the password (CRIT-06); `-recovery-file-out` is judged before anything is asked on each verb, both copies are voided when a restore does not take effect, and a second copy failing voids the first (FID-06); the help names `restore-key-file` and `-recovery-file-out`, and a stray key flag's refusal names the flags given (REG-04). A stated limit, ACCEPTED by the owner 2026-10-26: a second restore with no copy left on disk on a clock behind the first's can be outranked (FID-01, CRY-04; a skipped test records it). Residuals stated in the review: the folder swap is narrowed, not closed, without an `os.Root` (OUT-02); a never-backed-up recorded folder whose key files are gone is told to forget it, not offered the restore (FID-05 b, a design gap) | design-encrypted-archives-phase3 §3.1 K14, §9.2, §9.4, §9.5, R37 |
| MA-789 | S | encrypted archives Phase 3, P3-1a, no secret on the command line (§3.4, A26): a scan of every production Go file importing "flag" finds no flag whose name speaks of a password, a recovery key, a secret, a token, a key, a credential or a PIN unless it is one of the closed list that names a file or an environment variable holding it (`-password-file`, `-recovery-key-file`, `-recovery-key-out`, `-recovery-file-out`, `-client-secret-file`, `-client-secret-env`, `-token-cache`), each still defined — `-recovery-file-out` added in P3-1b: it names the file the recovery file is saved to, which opens nothing without the recovery key (the P3-1b pass, REG-05); the scan finds planted `-password` and `-api-token` flags | design-encrypted-archives-phase3 §3.4, A26, R37 |

| MA-555 | U | the Thunderbird pre-sync quiet detector (design section 4.4.2, the revision-3 L-M5 rule): quiet is declared only after at least one real change has been observed in the watched mail files since `up` AND then no change for `quiet`, so the ~10-minute opening silence of Thunderbird's first offline poll never ends the session early — a session with no change waits to `maxWait` and ends `ok-noactivity`, never at settle+quiet; file growth, an mtime-only touch, a new file, a rename and a deletion each count as a change; a change still in flight at `maxWait` is `not-quiet`; a poll gap over 30 s (suspend) resets the clock; the FNV-1a digest folds (relative path, size, mtime) and NEVER opens a file (a same-size, same-mtime content edit does not move it) nor follows a symlinked directory; and the walk is bounded — EVERY visited node (directories and specials, not only regular files) counts toward the entry cap, and a tree past the cap or past depth 16 reports truncation, so a directory-heavy hostile tree cannot evade the bound. | MA-565 | U | the pre-sync write-ahead state record (design section 4.6): it persists atomically at mode 0600 (temp + fsync + rename, so a crash leaves the old record or the new one, never a torn one), refuses a record over the 4 KiB bound rather than truncating it, tolerates unknown fields (a newer minor writer never strands an older reader), reads a newer `version` and corrupt JSON as unreadable (treated as absent, warned once), and holds NO hostname and NO wall-clock deadline (PC10/PC11 — either would strand a local record across a network rename or a clock step); the streak counters survive a restart and a missing file is the normal no-prior-session case. The read is hardened through `util.ReadSecureFile` (Lstat-first, `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, size-capped), so a planted FIFO, symlink or oversized file at the record path is refused as unreadable WITHOUT blocking or over-allocating (R32), as the sibling lockfile already is. | MA-921 | U | the whole-session watchdog (design-presync-attended §2.6, C-2, C-3, C-77; the parts slices AS1a and AS1b land — the Cancel-quit path lands with AS-Q): `sessionBudget()` is computed from the same constants the session uses (every bound on the longest path, a counted `probesOnPath`, and 60 s of slack for the folder walk), and the fake clock drives the longest unattended path — a stale lock, Thunderbird appearing as late as it can, nothing arriving, the raw count, a quit that never closes, each probe at or just under its bound — which fits with the slack left over; this also fixes T21 (the old 1440 s was shorter than that path). The watchdog returns at once with `cancelled` when the Stop ends or the run is cancelled, and at its budget with `internal-error`; either way it marks the session abandoned and cancels the session context, so a session waiting on it ends, an abandoned session that reaches the pre-quit function afterwards starts no quit request and saves no `quitting` record, a quit request in flight is cancelled with it, and Windows' `minimizeMain` wait ends (C-64). After a budget timeout the profile lock (the real `lockfile`) stays held through the caller's read and is freed only once BOTH the abandoned session has returned and the caller's `Release` has run, in either order; the next session then gets the lock, not `profile-busy`. *Amended 2026-10-30 (slice AS1b, C-74, C-50; nothing weakened):* the S6 Cancel path — an attended run's Cancel (cause `ErrCancelAndQuit`) at the first S6 pass, every probe at or just under its bound, a stale lock and the launcher at its bound, Thunderbird appearing at the very end of the 90 s await or never — fits the budget less its slack, from the same constants; with that cause the watchdog waits for the session's own bounded child poll (whose result, left open by rule, it then returns), while any other cause, or a Stop during the poll, makes it return at once, `cancelled`, with `Left` taken from where the session was (`may-be-starting`). A panic in the session after it took the profile lock ends it `internal-error` and hands its release on like any other end (to the once-both latch when abandoned, as the watchdog's `Release` when not), so the real lock is still held after the session returned and is freed only by the caller's `Release`. A run's append then starts a fresh history in a regular file, so the pipe never holds a run: a real `app.Run` with a pipe planted at the name finishes and releases the archive lock (a second run takes it and finishes too), and the Desktop's overview, which reads the history on every render, renders at once with an empty Recent backups card. The walk, which never opens a file, sorts what it sees by name into mail — an mbox (a file with no extension other than compaction's `nstmp`, or any file with a sibling `<name>.msf`, so a folder name with a dot counts), or a maildir message in `cur` or `new` — and everything else (`.msf` indexes, `msgFilterRules.dat`, `popstate.dat`, logs, feeds, a maildir's `tmp`, search copies, system files). The gate opens only when a file seen this session (as mail or not) is now a LARGER mail file, or a mail file never seen this session holds data at a place the previous poll walked; it stays shut for an index or filter touch, a shrink (compaction), a rename or move that keeps the size (matched to a mail file really gone since the last poll), a new empty folder, a mailbox that drops out and comes back, a dotted mailbox that becomes mail when its index is rebuilt, and a never-seen file past where a capped walk stopped or inside a folder it could not read (walk keys sort in the walk's own order, so the cap's frontier says exactly what was seen). Once open, any watched change still resets the quiet clock; a session that sees only index touches waits to the maximum and ends `ok-noactivity`, in both modes. | MA-935 | U | the pre-sync wait and sleep (the quiet-rule review of MA-932, 2026-10-31, findings logic-4, effects-2 and effects-4; design-thunderbird-presync §4.4.2, lab L-C3): a sleep is judged on wall time, from the end of one poll's walk to the start of the next — on macOS the monotonic clock stops in sleep, so an hour asleep reads as 10 s on it — and a gap over 30 s (stretched by as much as a poll slower than the default 10 s adds) restarts the quiet clock, so a wait does not end "synced" just after waking, before Thunderbird has reconnected; the maximum wait counts time awake (wall time less the time past that gap), so a computer that slept 8 hours mid-wait goes on waiting when it wakes and ends `ok-noactivity` only after 20 minutes awake, and the once-a-minute progress lines count minutes awake; a slow walk (25 s) is not a sleep and still reaches quiet. On Windows the session keeps the computer from idle sleep from the launch to its end (`SetThreadExecutionState`, the copy export's own request, now the shared `internal/keepawake`), and a session that launches nothing asks for nothing. Positive twins first: an unchanged watch with 150 s polls still ends `ok-noactivity` at 20 minutes and mail then quiet still ends ok. Not covered: a sleep inside a walk (a walk is normally well under a second), and a sleep the person forces on Windows (a lid closed), which the keep-awake does not stop and which can still trip the session watchdog on waking. The guide says the same (MA-945's amendment). Mutations: Windows told "asleep is skipped", Linux told it runs on waking, the Mac's old end note, and the end note unwired each turn the test red. Rows MA-29..MA-37 were added by the adversarial pass; see
`docs/review-adversarial.md` for the findings they encode. Rows MA-40..MA-44
cover the `reindex` self-repair subcommand (R13); MA-45..MA-50 cover the
`schedule` subcommand (R14); MA-51 covers the input discovery behind the CLI
`-auto` flag and the GUI auto-detect step; MA-52..MA-55 cover Evolution store
support (R15); MA-56 covers auto-discovery filtering out unreadable Outlook data
files (R1); MA-57 covers containing a go-pst open-time panic on a corrupt data
file (R10); MA-58..MA-60 cover the `-outlook` Outlook-COM PST export (R16) — the
COM behaviour itself is lab-tier (MA-60, pending a real Windows+Outlook box),
with the pure name-safety (MA-58) and the off-Windows refusal (MA-59) tested in
CI. MA-61..MA-64 cover the Microsoft Graph app-only server-side source (R17):
the client, the RunGraph pipeline+incremental, and the refusals are CI-tested
against an in-process fake Graph server; MA-64 (a real tenant) is lab-tier.
