(`docs/design-fault-isolation.md` §4.8.6–§4.8.7 and §9, ids from
`docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §13.1); each case that design keeps open is a
skip encoder citing its row. MA-402 is the extraction workspace's row
(`docs/design-extraction-workspace.md` §9), built in that slice, which also
lands R29 and S52. MA-400, MA-401, MA-403, MA-515, MA-527 and MA-528 are its
slice E1's (Graph additions, redirect and body bounds, F15, identity-safe token
write-back); MA-401 and MA-403 are the "a" halves, whose "b" halves are MA-505
(fault-isolation's FI-SGc) and MA-506 (E3).

MA-332, MA-333 (non-page), MA-334, MA-337, MA-338, MA-340 (bodies), MA-349; S3:
MA-335, MA-336, MA-340, MA-341; S4: MA-339, MA-333 (page), MA-348; S5: MA-342; S6:
MA-343; S7: MA-344, MA-345, MA-346 (b, d), MA-347 (repair half)). MA-343 is tier L
and pending: its lab story is the Outlook-on-Windows export the row states, and
S6 may not merge while it is L. Each row's Prove-fail mutation is folded into the
end of its Asserts cell, because this table has four columns. R27 cites R28, which
lands with re-capture integrity's slice S1. The fidelity corpus (`testdata/mime/`,
indexed by `corpus.tsv`, with the PST twin `testdata/support.pst`) and the identity
and content goldens recorded over it from the release build v0.7.1 are in place, with
their provenance in the goldens' headers; `TestMimeCorpusIsComplete` and
`TestMimeGoldensHoldForTheBuild` (citing R27) guard them until S1 points them at the
frozen replay. No corpus input panics the v1 parse, so MA-330's panicking-item case
stays open as a skip, and the identity golden pins the stub from a constructed
message. The design's R2 clarification (§6) does
not land here (ruling J7): it waits for ESC-2, as one joint paragraph with
re-capture integrity's R2 amendment.
Slice MF-S1 closes MA-347's identity half with every write recording `chash` (MC5)
and `parse_scheme` (MC12); MA-329's baseline `internal/app/testdata/p14-golden.json`
was regenerated with those two additive fields on the healthy records by the owner's
decision of 2026-10-11, and nothing else in it moved.
Slice MF-S2 (`docs/design-mime-fidelity.md` §10, rulings §9 row 20) lands the fidelity
walker, its parameter, text and transfer decoding, the gap classes and bounds (P2, P4
`.partial`, P5, P10, P13), the shared `internal/util` primitives (MC35), the report
kinds, counts and wording, and `BenchmarkParseCorpus` (MC39). It adds no row and no
R, S or X id: the rows are the S0 rows above. It turns MA-332, MA-333 (non-page),
MA-334, MA-337, MA-338, MA-340 (bodies) and MA-349 (parser bounds) into real tests and
MA-348's counts into a real test. What each of those rows leaves for a later slice is a
skip encoder citing the row: MA-333's page assertion (MF-S4), MA-340's headers (MF-S3),
MA-348's page blocks and repair figures (MF-S4, MF-S7) and MA-349's export-side recovers
(MF-S4, MF-S7). Identity stays the frozen replay's: no identity or content golden moves.
The walker is ON (owner-approved 2026-10-12, rulings §13.29): `fidelityContent` in
`internal/source/mimeroute.go` is true and `state.ParseSchemeCurrent` is 2 (the S2 notes
carry `state.ParseSchemeFidelity`). Switching on narrowed four assertions of MF-S1's tests
(`TestFidelityParserKeepsTheIdentityGoldenOnTheCorpus`'s "content from v1", "v1 panics"
and "the content parser panics" subtests, and `TestMixedVersionsKeepIdentityAndStems`)
and moved `parse_scheme` from 1 to 2 on the 46 records of MA-329's baseline, nothing
else; no identity golden moved. A message v1 panics on keeps the stub identity (its terms
carry `NoIdentityDate`; `TestAStubIdentityMessageKeepsItsDisplayDatesAndTheStubIdentity`).
A parse gap is a message-level `ParseGap` or an attachment's `GapKind`, filed as a
label in the record's own Missing or Terminal list (not as a `ReadGaps` label, whose KEEP
guard would hold back every re-read of such a record). The README documents the new
report kinds and `status -json` keys; the friction review and X-series check of the new
wording are owed (design §10).

MA-350, MA-351, MA-352, MA-355, MA-356, MA-357, MA-358, MA-359 and MA-361 are
re-capture integrity's slice S1, interim (`docs/design-recapture-integrity.md` §9
and §10, ids from `docs/owner-rulings-2026-10-09.md` §8.3, §8.4 and §13.1), which
also lands R28, S50 and S51 and closes R27's reference to R28. S1 builds no product
code and lands only the rows of the confirmed-open gaps, each a skip encoder citing
the existing invariant its defect breaks: F46 and the legacy re-examination
(MA-350, MA-351, MA-352, MA-355) and the immutable-id placement of §1.6 (MA-356)
cite R1, the preserved original of §1.5 (MA-357) cites R20, N2, N3 and the
unchanged-path window of §1.7 (MA-358, MA-359) cite R5, and F48 (MA-361) cites R13.
Each encoder builds its archive with the real pipeline and runs a healthy twin first
(green today), then asserts what the row requires of today's surface, so it goes
green when the gap is fixed and, with its skip removed, fails today for the reason
its skip message names; the kept, unverified and wording assertions of a row (`Kept`,
`Stats.Kept`, the report and summary lines) and the fault seams it names (the kill
points C1..C8, the injected ENOSPC, the probe's temp counter) are added by the slice
that builds them; so do the cases that need the inventory or the verdicts themselves
(the client-placeholder part, the frozen-fingerprint label drift, the relabelled item
matched by CRC, a refused earlier original, the DEMOTE half and the timeline stamps of
MA-356, case (c) of MA-357 and its carried pre-fixity original). The crash cases
(MA-358) observe the archive from inside the run, after a message is committed and
before the next checkpoint save, which is the state a hard kill there leaves, and
assert that witness first: the snapshot holds the stale lock file and the manifest
the run started from, and, with the index kept, none of the run's index batch. The
unchanged-path result (§1.7) goes with ESC-3.
The slices that turn them green: RI-S5 (MA-350, MA-351, MA-352, MA-355, MA-356, MA-357), RI-S4
(MA-358, MA-359; MA-358's same-path case waits on ESC-3) and RI-S2 (MA-361). MA-361
spans two slices: its `-vN` redaction and N2-adoption twins are skip encoders that
RI-S2 lands (ESC-2). The rows that specify new behaviour (MA-353, MA-354, MA-360,
MA-362, MA-363, MA-364) land with the slice that builds them (RC32). No existing
catalog text changes here: the R2, R13 and S24 amendments wait for ESC-2, R1's
added sentence lands with RI-S5, R5's with RI-S4 and only on ESC-3 (a), and S39 is
unchanged (J2). R27's reference to R28 now resolves.
